Mathlib.RingTheory.LaurentSeries

LaurentSeriesdefMathlib.RingTheory.LaurentSeries, type
Wording
type of formal Laurent series with coefficients in R (from the docstring)
English
Let $R$ be a set. Then a type of formal Laurent series with coefficients in $R$ is an element of the function that maps $R$ to the set of Hahn series of $\Integer$ and $R$.
Formula
$R \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \mathrm{LaurentSeries} (R):= \lambda R . \mathrm{HahnSeries} (\Integer , R)$
French
no reading
LaurentSeries.Cauchy.coeffdefMathlib.RingTheory.LaurentSeries, function or constant
Wording
the coefficient of h and x2 (from the name)
English
Assume that $\mathrm{Cauchy}$ holds for $x_{1}$. Let $x_{2}$ be an integer. Then the coefficient of $h$ and $x_{2}$ is an element of $K$.
Formula
$\mathrm{Cauchy} (x_{1}), x_{2} \in \Integer \vdash \operatorname{coeff} (h , x_{2})\in K$
French
Supposons que $\mathrm{Cauchy}$ tient pour $x_{1}$. Soit $x_{2}$ un entier. Alors le coefficient de $h$ et $x_{2}$ est un élément de $K$.
LaurentSeries.Cauchy.limitdefMathlib.RingTheory.LaurentSeries, function or constant
Wording
the limit of h (from the name)
English
Assume that $\mathrm{Cauchy}$ holds for $x_{1}$. Then the limit of $h$ is a type of formal Laurent series with coefficients in $K$.
Formula
$\mathrm{Cauchy} (x_{1})\vdash \operatorname{limit} (h)\in \mathrm{LaurentSeries} (K)$
French
no reading
LaurentSeries.LaurentSeriesAlgEquivdefMathlib.RingTheory.LaurentSeries, function or constant
Wording
the Laurent series algebra isomorphism of K (from the name)
English
Let $K$ be a set. Then the Laurent series algebra isomorphism of $K$ is an algebra isomorphism of the set of types of formal Laurent series with coefficients in $K$ and the set of rational number function adic complements of $K$.
Formula
$K \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{LaurentSeriesAlgEquiv} (K)\in \mathrm{AlgEquiv} (K , \mathrm{LaurentSeries} (K), \operatorname{RatFuncAdicCompl} (K))$
French
no reading
LaurentSeries.LaurentSeriesPkgdefMathlib.RingTheory.LaurentSeries, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $K$ be a set. Then $\operatorname{LaurentSeriesPkg}$ applied to $K$ is an abstract completion of $\mathrm{WithVal}$ applied to the polynomial valuation of $K$.
Formula
$K \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{LaurentSeriesPkg} (K)\in \mathrm{AbstractCompletion} (\mathrm{WithVal} (\operatorname{polynomialValuationX} (K)))$
French
Soit $K$ un ensemble. Alors $\operatorname{LaurentSeriesPkg}$ appliqué à $K$ est une complétion abstraite de $\mathrm{WithVal}$ appliqué à la valuation polynomiale de $K$.
LaurentSeries.LaurentSeriesRingEquivdefMathlib.RingTheory.LaurentSeries, function or constant
Wording
the Laurent series ring isomorphism of K (from the name)
English
Let $K$ be a set. Then the Laurent series ring isomorphism of $K$ is a ring isomorphism of the set of types of formal Laurent series with coefficients in $K$ and the set of rational number function adic complements of $K$ defined as the inverse of $\operatorname{ratfuncAdicComplRingEquiv}$ applied to $K$.
Formula
$K \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{LaurentSeriesRingEquiv} (K):= \operatorname{symm} (\operatorname{ratfuncAdicComplRingEquiv} (K))\in \mathrm{RingEquiv} (\mathrm{LaurentSeries} (K), \operatorname{RatFuncAdicCompl} (K))$
French
no reading
LaurentSeries.RatFuncAdicCompldefMathlib.RingTheory.LaurentSeries, type
Wording
rational number function adic complement of K (from the name)
English
Let $K$ be a set. Then a rational number function adic complement of $K$ is an element of the function that maps $K$ to the set of adic completions of the set of rational number functions of $K$ and the ideal of $K$.
Formula
$K \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{RatFuncAdicCompl} (K):= \lambda K . \operatorname{adicCompletion} (\mathrm{RatFunc} (K), \operatorname{idealX} (K))$
French
Soit $K$ un ensemble. Alors un complément adique de fonctions de nombres rationnel de $K$ est un élément de la fonction qui envoie $K$ à l'ensemble de complétions adiques de l'ensemble de fonctions de nombres rationnelles de $K$ et l'idéal de $K$.
LaurentSeries.comparePkgdefMathlib.RingTheory.LaurentSeries, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $K$ be a set. Then $\operatorname{comparePkg}$ applied to $K$ is a uniform equivalence of the set of rational number function adic complements of $K$ and the set of types of formal Laurent series with coefficients in $K$ defined as the composition of the uniform equivalence of the set of rational number functions of $K$ and the ideal of $K$ and $\operatorname{compareEquiv}$ applied to $\operatorname{ratfuncAdicComplPkg}$ and $\operatorname{LaurentSeriesPkg}$ applied to $K$.
Formula
$K \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{comparePkg} (K):= \operatorname{trans} (\operatorname{uniformEquiv} (\mathrm{RatFunc} (K), \operatorname{idealX} (K)), \operatorname{compareEquiv} (\operatorname{ratfuncAdicComplPkg} , \operatorname{LaurentSeriesPkg} (K)))\in \mathrm{UniformEquiv} (\operatorname{RatFuncAdicCompl} (K), \mathrm{LaurentSeries} (K))$
French
no reading
LaurentSeries.derivativedefMathlib.RingTheory.LaurentSeries, function or constant
Wording
the derivative of R (from the docstring)
English
Let $R$ be a set. Then the derivative of $R$ is a linear map of the set of types of formal Laurent series with coefficients in $V$ and the set of types of formal Laurent series with coefficients in $V$ defined as the Hasse derivative of $R$ and $1$.
Formula
$R \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{derivative} (R):= \operatorname{hasseDeriv} (R , 1)\in \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (R), \mathrm{LaurentSeries} (V), \mathrm{LaurentSeries} (V))$
French

LaurentSeries.extensionAsRingHomdefMathlib.RingTheory.LaurentSeries, function or constant
Wording
the extension as ring homomorphism of K and hf (from the name)
English
Let $K$ be a set such that $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to the composition of the algebra map of the set of rational number functions of $K$ and the set of types of formal Laurent series with coefficients in $K$ and the ring homomorphism of the equivalence of the polynomial valuation of $K$ is continuous. Then the extension as ring homomorphism of $K$ and $\mathrm{hf}$ is a ring homomorphism of the set of completions of $\mathrm{WithVal}$ applied to the polynomial valuation of $K$ and the set of types of formal Laurent series with coefficients in $K$ defined as the completion extension of the composition of the algebra map of the set of rational number functions of $K$ and the set of types of formal Laurent series with coefficients in $K$ and the ring homomorphism of the equivalence of the polynomial valuation of $K$ and $\mathrm{hf}$.
Formula
$K \in \mathrm{Set} , \mathrm{Continuous} (\operatorname{coe} (\operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{algebraMap} (\mathrm{RatFunc} (K), \mathrm{LaurentSeries} (K)), \operatorname{toRingHom} (\operatorname{equiv} (\operatorname{polynomialValuationX} (K))))))\vdash \operatorname{extensionAsRingHom} (K , \mathrm{hf}):= \operatorname{extensionHom} (\operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{algebraMap} (\mathrm{RatFunc} (K), \mathrm{LaurentSeries} (K)), \operatorname{toRingHom} (\operatorname{equiv} (\operatorname{polynomialValuationX} (K)))), \mathrm{hf})\in \mathrm{RingHom} (\operatorname{Completion} (\mathrm{WithVal} (\operatorname{polynomialValuationX} (K))), \mathrm{LaurentSeries} (K))$
French
no reading
LaurentSeries.hasseDerivdefMathlib.RingTheory.LaurentSeries, function or constant
Wording
the Hasse derivative of R and k (from the name)
English
Let $R$ be a set. Let $k$ be a natural number. Then the Hasse derivative of $R$ and $k$ is a linear map of the set of types of formal Laurent series with coefficients in $V$ and the set of types of formal Laurent series with coefficients in $V$.
Formula
$R \in \mathrm{Set} , k \in \Natural \vdash \operatorname{hasseDeriv} (R , k)\in \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (R), \mathrm{LaurentSeries} (V), \mathrm{LaurentSeries} (V))$

Formula
$K \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{powerSeriesAlgEquiv} (K)\in \mathrm{AlgEquiv} (K , \mathrm{PowerSeries} (K), \SubtypeSet {x_{1}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoAA {\operatorname{adicCompletionIntegers} (\mathrm{RatFunc} (K), \operatorname{idealX} (K))}{x_{1}})})$

Wording
the power series part of x1 (from the name)
English
Let $x_{1}$ be a type of formal Laurent series with coefficients in $R$. Then the power series part of $x_{1}$ is a power series of $R$ defined as $\operatorname{mk}$ applied to the function that maps $n$ to the coefficient function of $x_{1}$ and the sum of the order of $x_{1}$ and $n$.
Formula
$x_{1} \in \mathrm{LaurentSeries} (R)\vdash \mathrm{PowerSeries} (R)$

Let $K$ be a set. PowerSeries.idealXdefMathlib.RingTheory.LaurentSeries, function or constant
Wording
the prime ideal of K (from the docstring)
English
Let $K$ be a set. Then the prime ideal of $K$ is a height one spectrum of the set of power series of $K$.
Formula
$K \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{idealX} (K)\in \operatorname{HeightOneSpectrum} (\mathrm{PowerSeries} (K))$
French
Soit $K$ un ensemble. Alors l'idéal premier de $K$ est un spectre de one d'hauteurs de l'ensemble de séries de puissances de $K$.
RatFunc.polynomialValuationXdefMathlib.RingTheory.LaurentSeries, function or constant
Wording
the polynomial valuation of K (from the name)
English
Let $K$ be a set. Then the polynomial valuation of $K$ is a valuation of the set of rational number functions of $K$ and $\mathrm{WithZero}$ applied to the set of multiplicatives of $\Integer$ defined as the valuation of the set of rational number functions of $K$ and the ideal of $K$.
Formula
$K \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{polynomialValuationX} (K):= \operatorname{valuation} (\mathrm{RatFunc} (K), \operatorname{idealX} (K))\in \mathrm{Valuation} (\mathrm{RatFunc} (K), \mathrm{WithZero} (\mathrm{Multiplicative} (\Integer)))$
French
Soit $K$ un ensemble. Alors la valuation polynomiale de $K$ est une valuation de l'ensemble de fonctions de nombres rationnelles de $K$ et $\mathrm{WithZero}$ appliqué à l'ensemble de multiplicative de $\Integer$ défini comme la valuation de l'ensemble de fonctions de nombres rationnelles de $K$ et l'idéal de $K$.

Module.lengthdefMathlib.RingTheory.Length, function or constant
Wording
the length of R and M (from the docstring)
English
Let $R$ and $M$ be sets. Then the length of $R$ and $M$ is a natural number.
Formula
$R , M \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{length} (R , M)\in \mathrm{ENat}$
French
Soient $R$ et $M$ des ensembles. Alors la longueur de $R$ et $M$ est un nombre naturel.

Mathlib.RingTheory.LinearDisjoint

Subalgebra.LinearDisjointdefMathlib.RingTheory.LinearDisjoint, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $A$ and $B$ be subalgebras of $R$ and $S$. Then $\operatorname{LinearDisjoint}$ holds for $A$ and $B$, if $\operatorname{LinearDisjoint}$ holds for $\operatorname{toSubmodule}$ applied to $A$ and $\operatorname{toSubmodule}$ applied to $B$.
Formula
$A , B \in \mathrm{Subalgebra} (R , S)\vdash \operatorname{LinearDisjoint} (A , B):\Leftrightarrow \operatorname{LinearDisjoint} (\operatorname{toSubmodule} (A), \operatorname{toSubmodule} (B))$
French
Soient $A$ et $B$ des sous-algèbres de $R$ et $S$. Alors $\operatorname{LinearDisjoint}$ tient pour $A$ et $B$ si $\operatorname{LinearDisjoint}$ tient pour $\operatorname{toSubmodule}$ appliqué à $A$ et $\operatorname{toSubmodule}$ appliqué à $B$.
Subalgebra.LinearDisjoint.basisOfBasisLeftdefMathlib.RingTheory.LinearDisjoint, function or constant
Wording
the basis of #3 (from the name)
English
Assume that $\operatorname{LinearDisjoint}$ holds for $A$ and $B$. Assume that $\MaxmaxMACRoAF {A}{B}$ is equal to the top. Let $b$ be a basis of $R$ and $\SubtypeSet {x_{5}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoT {A}{x_{5}})}$. Then the basis of $b$ is a basis of $\SubtypeSet {x_{4}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoT {B}{x_{4}})}$ and $S$.
Formula
$\operatorname{LinearDisjoint} (A , B), \EqtMACRoJ {\MaxmaxMACRoAM {A}{B}}{\operatorname{top}}, b \in \operatorname{Basis} (x_{3} , R , \SubtypeSet {x_{5}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoAA {A}{x_{5}})})\vdash \operatorname{basisOfBasisLeft} (H , \mathrm{H'} , b)\in \operatorname{Basis} (x_{3} , \SubtypeSet {x_{4}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoAA {B}{x_{4}})}, S)$
French
Supposons que $\operatorname{LinearDisjoint}$ tient pour $A$ et $B$. Supposons que $\MaxmaxMACRoAF {A}{B}$ est égal au top. Soit $b$ une base de $R$ et $\SubtypeSet {x_{5}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoT {A}{x_{5}})}$. Alors la base de $b$ est une base de $\SubtypeSet {x_{4}}{(\MembershipmemMACRoT {B}{x_{4}})}$ et $S$.

Mathlib.RingTheory.LocalProperties.Basic

LocalizationPreservesdefMathlib.RingTheory.LocalProperties.Basic, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $P$ be a function from sets $R$ and elements of $\mathrm{Proof}$ applied to the proposition that $R$ is a commutative ring to propositions. Then $\mathrm{LocalizationPreserves}$ holds for $R$ and $P$, if for all sets $R$, for all elements $\mathrm{hR}$ of $\mathrm{CommRing}$ applied to $R$, for all submonoids $M$ of $R$, for all sets $S$, for all elements $\mathrm{hS}$ of $\mathrm{CommRing}$ applied to $S$, for all elements $x$ of $\mathrm{Algebra}$ applied to $R$ and $S$, if $M$ is a localization, then if $R$ holds for $R$ and $\mathrm{hR}$, then $R$ holds for $S$ and $\mathrm{hS}$.
Formula
$P \in \mathrm{Set} \rightarrow \mathrm{Proof} (\mathrm{CommRing} (R))\rightarrow \mathrm{Prop} \vdash \mathrm{LocalizationPreserves} (R , P):\Leftrightarrow \forall R \in \mathrm{Set} . \forall \mathrm{hR} \in \mathrm{CommRing} (R). \forall M \in \mathrm{Submonoid} (R). \forall S \in \mathrm{Set} . \forall \mathrm{hS} \in \mathrm{CommRing} (S). \forall x \in \mathrm{Algebra} (R , S). \mathrm{IsLocalization} (M , S)\rightarrow R (R , \mathrm{hR})\rightarrow R (S , \mathrm{hS})$
French
Soit $P$ une fonction des ensembles $R$ et des éléments de $\mathrm{Proof}$ appliqué à la proposition que $R$ est un anneau commutatif à des propositions. Alors $\mathrm{LocalizationPreserves}$ tient pour $R$ et $P$ si pour tous les ensembles $R$, pour tous les éléments $\mathrm{hR}$ de $\mathrm{CommRing}$ appliqué à $R$, pour tous les sous-monoïdes $M$ de $R$, pour tous les ensembles $S$, pour tous les éléments $\mathrm{hS}$ de $\mathrm{CommRing}$ appliqué à $S$, pour tous les éléments $x$ de $\mathrm{Algebra}$ appliqué à $R$ et $S$, si $M$ est une localisation, alors si $R$ tient pour $R$ et $\mathrm{hR}$, alors $R$ tient pour $S$ et $\mathrm{hS}$.

Wording
no wording
English
Let $P$ be a function from sets $R$ and elements of $\mathrm{Proof}$ applied to the proposition that $R$ is a commutative ring to propositions. Then $\mathrm{OfLocalizationMaximal}$ holds for $R$ and $P$, if for all sets $R$, for all elements $x$ of $\mathrm{CommRing}$ applied to $R$, if for all ideals $J$ of $R$, for all elements $h_{1}$ of $\mathrm{Proof}$ applied to the proposition that $J$ is maximal, $R$ holds for $\operatorname{AtPrime}$ applied to $J$ and $\operatorname{instCommRing}$, then $R$ holds for $R$ and $x$.

Then $R$ is a localization span target, if for all sets $R$ and $S$, for all elements $x$ of $\mathrm{CommRing}$ applied to $R$, for all elements $x_{1}$ of $\mathrm{CommRing}$ applied to $S$, for all ring homomorphisms $f$ of $R$ and $S$, for all elements $s$ of $\wp S$, if the span of $s$ is equal to the top, then if for all elements $r$ of $s$, $R$ holds for $R$, $\operatorname{Away}$ applied to $r$, $x$, $\operatorname{instCommRing}$ and the composition of the algebra map of $S$ and $\operatorname{Away}$ applied to $r$ and $f$, then $R$ holds for $R$, $S$, $x$, $x_{1}$ and $f$.

$P \in \mathrm{Set} \rightarrow \mathrm{Set} \rightarrow \mathrm{Proof} (\mathrm{CommRing} (R))\rightarrow \mathrm{Proof} (\mathrm{CommRing} (S))\rightarrow \mathrm{RingHom} (R , S)\rightarrow \mathrm{Prop} \vdash \operatorname{OfLocalizationSpanTarget} (R , S , x , x_{1} , P):\Leftrightarrow \forall R \in \mathrm{Set} . \forall S \in \mathrm{Set} . \forall x \in \mathrm{CommRing} (R). \forall x_{1} \in \mathrm{CommRing} (S). \forall f \in \mathrm{RingHom} (R , S). \forall s \in \wp S . \EqtMACRoJ {\operatorname{span} (s)}{\operatorname{top}}\rightarrow (\forall r \in \operatorname{Elem} (s). French

Ideal.ResidueField.liftdefMathlib.RingTheory.LocalRing.ResidueField.Ideal, function or constant
Wording
the lift of I and f (from the name)
English
Let $I$ be an ideal of $R$. Let $f$ be a ring homomorphism of $R$ and $S$ such that $I$ is less than or equal to the kernel of $f$ and the complement of $I$ is less than or equal to the preimage of $f$ and the submonoid consisting of the units of $S$. Then the lift of $I$ and $f$ is a ring homomorphism of the set of residue fields of $I$ and $S$.
Formula
$I \in \mathrm{Ideal} (R), f \in \mathrm{RingHom} (R , S), I \leq \operatorname{ker} (f), \operatorname{primeCompl} (I)\leq \operatorname{comap} (f , \operatorname{submonoid} (S))\vdash \operatorname{lift} (I , f , h_{1} , h_{2})\in \mathrm{RingHom} (\operatorname{ResidueField} (I), S)$
French

Alors le lift de $I$ et $f$ est un homomorphisme d'anneaux de l'ensemble de corps de résidus de $I$ et $S$.

Let $p$ be an ideal of $R$. Let $x_{1}$ be a set. Then the equivalence quotient maximal ideal of $p$ and $x_{1}$ is an algebra isomorphism of the set of quotients of $R$ and $p$ and the set of quotients of $x_{1}$ and the maximal ideal of $x_{1}$.
Formula
$p \in \mathrm{Ideal} (R), x_{1} \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{equivQuotMaximalIdeal} (p , x_{1})\in \mathrm{AlgEquiv} (R , \operatorname{Quotient} (R , p), \operatorname{Quotient} (x_{1} , \operatorname{maximalIdeal} (x_{1})))$
French
Soit $p$ un idéal de $R$. Soit $x_{1}$ un ensemble. Alors l'idéal maximal de quotients d'équivalences de $p$ et $x_{1}$ est un isomorphisme d'algèbres de l'ensemble de quotients de $R$ et $p$ et l'ensemble de quotients de $x_{1}$ et l'idéal maximal de $x_{1}$.

Let $x$ and $R$ be sets. Let $n$ be a natural number. Then the truncation total algebra homomorphism of $n$ is an algebra homomorphism of the set of multivariate power series of $x$ and $R$ and the set of quotients of the set of multivariate polynomials of $x$ and $R$ and the exponentiation of the ideal spanned by all variables of $x$ and $R$ to $n$.
Formula
$x , R \in \mathrm{Set} , n \in \Natural \vdash \operatorname{truncTotalAlgHom} (x , R , n)\in \mathrm{AlgHom} (\mathrm{MvPolynomial} (x , R), \mathrm{MvPowerSeries} (x , R), \operatorname{Quotient} (\mathrm{MvPolynomial} (x , R), \operatorname{idealOfVars} (x , R)^ {n}))$
French
Soient $x$ et $R$ des ensembles. Soit $n$ un nombre naturel. Alors l'homomorphisme d'algèbres total de truncation de $n$ est un homomorphisme d'algèbres de l'ensemble de séries de puissances multivariées de $x$ et $R$ et l'ensemble de quotients de l'ensemble de polynômes multivariés de $x$ et $R$ et la puissance du [DepNoun] de $x$ et $R$ en $n$.

Wording
the multivariate power series of i (from the name)

Formula

Mathlib.RingTheory.MvPowerSeries.GaussNorm

MvPowerSeries.AchievesGaussNormdefMathlib.RingTheory.MvPowerSeries.GaussNorm, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $v$ be a function from elements of $R$ to real numbers. Let $c$ be a function from elements of $x$ to real numbers. Let $f$ be a multivariate power series of $x$ and $R$. Let $i$ be an element of $\FinsuppMACRoQ {x}{\Natural}$. Then $\operatorname{AchievesGaussNorm}$ holds for $v$, $c$, $f$ and $i$, if $v (\operatorname{coeff} (i , f))\times \FinsuppProd {i}{x_{2}}{x_{3}}{(c (x_{2})^ {x_{3}})}$ is equal to the Gauss norm of $c$ and $f$.
Formula
$v \in R \rightarrow \Real , c \in x \rightarrow \Real , f \in \mathrm{MvPowerSeries} (x , R), i \in \FinsuppMACRoX {x}{\Natural}\vdash \operatorname{AchievesGaussNorm} (v , c , f , i):\Leftrightarrow v (\operatorname{coeff} (i , f))\times \FinsuppProd {i}{x_{2}}{x_{3}}{(c (x_{2})^ {x_{3}})}= \operatorname{gaussNorm} (v , c , f)$
French
Soit $v$ une fonction des éléments de $R$ à des nombres réels. Soit $c$ une fonction des éléments de $x$ à des nombres réels. Soit $f$ une série de puissances multivariée de $x$ et $R$. Soit $i$ un élément de $\FinsuppMACRoQ {x}{\Natural}$. Alors $\operatorname{AchievesGaussNorm}$ tient pour $v$, $c$, $f$ et $i$ si $v (\operatorname{coeff} (i , f))\times \FinsuppProd {i}{x_{2}}{x_{3}}{(c (x_{2})^ {x_{3}})}$ est égal à la norme de Gauss de $c$ et $f$.
MvPowerSeries.HasGaussNormdefMathlib.RingTheory.MvPowerSeries.GaussNorm, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $v$ be a function from elements of $R$ to real numbers. Let $c$ be a function from elements of $x$ to real numbers. Let $f$ be a multivariate power series of $x$ and $R$. Then $\operatorname{HasGaussNorm}$ holds for $v$, $c$ and $f$, if the range of the function that maps $t$ to $v (\operatorname{coeff} (t , f))\times \FinsuppProd {t}{x_{2}}{x_{3}}{(c (x_{2})^ {x_{3}})}$ is bounded above.
Formula
$v \in R \rightarrow \Real , c \in x \rightarrow \Real , f \in \mathrm{MvPowerSeries} (x , R)\vdash \operatorname{HasGaussNorm} (v , c , f):\Leftrightarrow \mathrm{BddAbove} (\operatorname{range} (\lambda t . v (\operatorname{coeff} (t , f))\times \FinsuppProd {t}{x_{2}}{x_{3}}{(c (x_{2})^ {x_{3}})}))$
French
Soit $v$ une fonction des éléments de $R$ à des nombres réels. Soit $c$ une fonction des éléments de $x$ à des nombres réels. Soit $f$ une série de puissances multivariée de $x$ et $R$. Alors $\operatorname{HasGaussNorm}$ tient pour $v$, $c$ et $f$ si l'image de la fonction qui envoie $t$ à $v (\operatorname{coeff} (t , f))\times \FinsuppProd {t}{x_{2}}{x_{3}}{(c (x_{2})^ {x_{3}})}$ est majorée.
MvPowerSeries.gaussNormdefMathlib.RingTheory.MvPowerSeries.GaussNorm, function or constant
Wording
the Gauss norm of c and f (from the name)
English
Let $v$ be a function from elements of $R$ to real numbers. Let $c$ be a function from elements of $x$ to real numbers. Let $f$ be a multivariate power series of $x$ and $R$. Then the Gauss norm of $c$ and $f$ is a real number defined as the supremum of the function that maps $t$ to $v (\operatorname{coeff} (t , f))\times \FinsuppProd {t}{x_{2}}{x_{3}}{(c (x_{2})^ {x_{3}})}$.
Formula
$v \in R \rightarrow \Real , c \in x \rightarrow \Real , f \in \mathrm{MvPowerSeries} (x , R)\vdash \operatorname{gaussNorm} (v , c , f):= \mathrm{iSup} (\lambda t . v (\operatorname{coeff} (t , f))\times \FinsuppProd {t}{x_{2}}{x_{3}}{(c (x_{2})^ {x_{3}})})\in \Real$
French
Soit $v$ une fonction des éléments de $R$ à des nombres réels. Soit $c$ une fonction des éléments de $x$ à des nombres réels. Soit $f$ une série de puissances multivariée de $x$ et $R$. Alors la norme de Gauss de $c$ et $f$ est un nombre réel défini comme le supremum de la fonction qui envoie $t$ à $v (\operatorname{coeff} (t , f))\times \FinsuppProd {t}{x_{2}}{x_{3}}{(c (x_{2})^ {x_{3}})}$.

$s \in \wp R , \mathrm{sm} \in \mathrm{Subsemigroup} (R), \mathrm{sa} \in \mathrm{AddSubgroup} (R), \EqtMACRoJ {\mathrm{sm}}{s}, \EqtMACRoJ {\mathrm{sa}}{s}\vdash \operatorname{mk'} (s , \mathrm{sm} , \mathrm{sa} , \mathrm{hm} , \mathrm{ha})\in \mathrm{NonUnitalSubring} (R)$
French
Soit $s$ un élément de $\wp R$. Soit $\mathrm{sm}$ un sous-demi-groupe de $R$. Soit $\mathrm{sa}$ un sous-groupe additif de $R$. Supposons que $\EqtMACRoH {\mathrm{sm}}{s}$. Supposons que $\EqtMACRoH {\mathrm{sa}}{s}$. Alors $\operatorname{mk'}$ appliqué à $s$, $\mathrm{sm}$, $\mathrm{sa}$, $\mathrm{hm}$ et $\mathrm{ha}$ est un sous-anneau non unitaire de $R$.
